...to banish the coarseness <note>original: Roheit; referring to a lack of refinement or the "primitive" state of society before Enlightenment</note> of the customs of their century, and to transform the peoples into civilized nations. "Very well, dear Doctor," said the yawning Shah Gebal—"but to the point, if I may ask—"

Danischmende, who was already accustomed to such interruptions <note>original: Einfälle; literally "ideas" or "notions," but here referring to the Sultan's impatient outbursts</note>, merely bowed deeply—and continued undisturbed—

"It was one of the most ingenious ideas of the ancients to make use of the human inclination toward the marvelous <note>original: zum Wunderbaren; the human tendency to be fascinated by the supernatural or the extraordinary</note> in order to lead them toward wisdom and virtue. For this reason, they established certain stages <note>original: Stuffen; degrees or levels of progression within a secret society</note> and ceremonies which no one who wished to be initiated could evade: this..."

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the origins of the Knights Templar, following the mention of "Masons and Templars" at the end of the previous page. The speaker, likely the sage Danishmende, is explaining the Crusades to an Eastern audience using their own cultural vocabulary.</meta>

# 69
A 5

"The Templars? The Templars?" asked the curious Nurmahal. —

"Yes, my fair lady—these people were the better-thinking part among those visionary fanatics who bound themselves together to tear a piece of sandy desert, which concealed the tomb of their Prophet <note>original: "ihres Propheten" – referring to Jesus Christ; the narrator uses Islamic terminology to describe Christian figures to suit the fictional Eastern setting</note>, from the hands of its owners out of holy religious zeal. A poor dervish <note>likely Peter the Hermit (c. 1050–1115), a priest who was a key figure in inciting the First Crusade</note>, who instead of preaching humility and peace, instilled this idea of conquest and encouraged the peoples of Europe to murder, was responsible for the deaths of 2,350,000 unfortunate souls who found their lives ended in the sandy deserts before Palestine. Their Prophet would not—or could not—support the rescuers of his grave in their glorious intention, and so this holy purpose was thwarted."

"Despite all my inclination for the honor of my Prophet," said Shah Gebal, "I would still have had this dervish hanged if it had occurred to him to preach murder out of religious zeal in my states as well—"

<summary>In this passage, the narrator provides a critical Enlightenment-era perspective on the Crusades, characterizing the movement as a tragic waste of life sparked by fanatical preaching. The speaker uses Islamic titles like "dervish" and "prophet" to describe Christian figures, highlighting the shared nature of religious extremism across cultures.</summary>

<keywords>Knights Templar, Crusades, Peter the Hermit, Palestine, Shah Gebal, religious fanaticism, Enlightenment philosophy</keywords>

These unfortunate knights, who suspected their imminent downfall, hid their writings and knowledge, and some of the survivors collected these and continued their society under another name. —

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the mystic Aeschmende (Aeschm.) and the philosopher Danischmende. "Splendid! Excellent, Doctor," said Danischmende; "and what of your <term>Kabbalah<note>Original: "Cabala." A tradition of Jewish mysticism. In the 18th-century context of this story, it refers to the "Christian Kabbalah" practiced by occultists who claimed they could use secret letter-arrangements to perform magic or predict the future.</note></term>?"

Aeschm. "Yes! As for that—I could tell you wonders. Through it, we read all the actions of men—all the events of the future—though, to be sure, our <term>oracles<note>Ancient oracles were famous for being "Loxian" or ambiguous; Danischmende argues this vagueness is a tool for deception rather than divine communication.</note></term>, like all prophecies in general, are always very obscure.—"

Danischmende. "And for that very reason, they are useless and a gross deception. What does it mean to 'read' in the future? Is it not the greatest contradiction? The future does not exist—it is not yet here—how do you intend to see now what is not there?—and furthermore, I challenge anyone to show me even a single prophecy that was even slightly intelligible. Does it not amount to blaspheming the Deity to suggest that He would choose to speak to us only to enjoy our embarrassment? Either God allows man to learn his future fate, or He does not: in the former case, why are the oracles obscure? In the second,"

<summary>Danischmende challenges the logic of prophecy and the Kabbalah, arguing that predicting a non-existent future is a logical impossibility and that vague oracles are a form of divine mockery.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the philosopher Danischmende and the mystic Aeschmende, in the presence of the monarch Shah Gebal. The catchword from the previous page was "wer," which likely completed the sentence "who/which [would enjoy our embarrassment]."</meta>

why do such things exist? This childish <term>science<note>Original: "Wissenschaft." In the 18th century, this term was applied broadly to any organized body of knowledge, including those we now consider pseudosciences like alchemy or Kabbalah.</note></term>, Sire, arises from the natural, restless urge of man to see himself outside the circle of the present, in order to hope for a better fate. Man has sought to make a <term>system<note>Original: "Sistem." Danischmende is critiquing the Enlightenment obsession with creating "systems" or logical frameworks for everything, arguing that applying logic to "conjecture" only leads to deeper error.</note></term> out of the art of conjecture, and has fallen into the gravest errors.

Generally, however, Sire, it is dangerous to tolerate such <term>Kabbalists<note>Followers of a mystical tradition; here used as a catch-all for occultists who claim secret divine knowledge.</note></term> in large civil societies: for when a malicious <term>fanatic<note>Original: "Schwärmer." A derogatory 18th-century term for someone who is overly enthusiastic or "delirious" about religious or mystical ideas, often implying they are a threat to social order.</note></term> can beguile credulous people and bring them to firmly believe that the Deity speaks through him, it is clear that if his interest should demand it, this Deity will suggest to him <term>regicide<note>The act of killing a king. "I will not suffer them in my states for another hour," said Shah Gebal—who was already feeling anxious and afraid—"I will not tolerate them—they shall all—all be gone!"

**Aeschm.** But—Sire!—

No "but"!—The proof from Danischmende is as clear as the multiplication tables;

People always couple the thought of a "miracle" with a "supernatural thing." But they do not consider that this is as much as saying: an "impossible thing." For what is understood by the word "Nature"? Certainly nothing other than the eternal arrangement of things. Thus, a miracle within this arrangement would be an impossibility. And for the most part, these so-called miracles are useless, and their cause is ridiculous. against the proposition presented to me.       This belief in **angels** and **devils** comes from the general ancient doctrine that the world is ruled by invisible beings — a natural, crude idea of uneducated **primitive people**: invisible beings, greater and more powerful than they were, rolled the thunder — hurled the lightning — and shook the earth. Even man himself, swept away by the rapid current of time, stands with restless gazes at the time that carries him and the distance he has covered: he would like to extend his gaze to the furthest limits of his path, consult the mirror of the future, and see through the entire chain of his **existence** with a single glance.

--- Page 61 ---
<meta>This page continues the critique of mystical thinking by the character Danisch. It picks up from the previous page's discussion on the human desire to penetrate the mysteries of existence and the future.</meta>

This restless desire gives birth to all **fanatical phenomena** <note>originally: "schwärmerische Phänomena"; in the 18th century, *Schwärmerei* (fanaticism or enthusiasm) was a derogatory term used by Enlightenment thinkers to describe religious or mystical delusions where imagination overwhelms reason</note>: the ever-active **imagination** always attempts to tear the **veil of the future**, and man becomes a **visionary** <note>originally: "Schwärmer"; one who is caught up in irrational mystical impulses</note> because he cannot become a **prophet**.

The inclination toward the **wondrous** implanted within us is sufficient, despite all the propositions of **skepticism**, to immortalize our **credulity**: the imagination, when left to its own devices, loves to heap wonder upon wonder, and then defends against the **philosopher** the monsters it has birthed, simply because they are its own work.

**Lawgivers** and **sectarians** <note>or cult leaders; those who lead religious factions</note> knew that one can always lead mankind if one displays great, wondrous spectacles before them, and surprises their **imagination** more than enlightening their **reason**. From admiration to a **fanatical faith** is but an imperceptible step: the philosopher alone doubts, because he is wise enough not to trust what he sees and hears, and even less the works of his own imagination.

...to soothe their conscience. I leave it to everyone to see for themselves the evil consequences of such principles, and to conclude how dangerous they could be in the hands of **villains** <note>originally: "Bösewichtern"; people of malicious or wicked character.</note>. 

What is particularly noteworthy is that when a **new initiate** <note>originally: "Neuaufgenommener"</note> has previously been initiated into other **societies** <term>Gesellschaften; this refers to the complex web of 18th-century secret societies, such as the Freemasons or Illuminati, which often competed for the same members.</term> and possesses certain **secret degrees** whose disclosure is forbidden by heavy oaths—or when someone has received a **secret** under the obligation of his **word of honor** <note>originally: "Ehrenwort"</note> to share it with no one else—these people, ever spying on every secret to gain more influence for themselves, eagerly wish to investigate and know them. Thus, they declare that the Superiors have received the right from God to absolve every person from whatever oaths and duties they may have. They present such an **absolution** <term>Absolution; traditionally a priestly act of forgiving sins, here used by the Order's leaders to "cancel" a member's previous legal or moral oaths to other organizations.</term> to the **newly initiated** and, in this manner, trick gullible people out of everything they want. I consider this "right" to be one of the most dangerous in **civil societies**!

<summary>The author warns that the Rosicrucian leadership claims a divine right to absolve members of any previous oaths or promises of secrecy. This allows the Order to extract confidential information from other organizations, a practice the author deems a grave threat to social and legal order.</summary>

This Order has nine degrees, all of which are paid for very dearly. \*) The last, or ninth level, costs 99 **marks** <note>A "mark" was a traditional unit of weight for precious metals, roughly equivalent to 230 grams.</note> of **fine gold** <note>originally: "löthigen Goldes"; gold of high purity.</note>, a sum which will certainly seem small when one considers that through it, one is transformed into a true **Magus** <term>Magus; a high-level initiate or sorcerer who has attained profound wisdom and mastery over the natural and supernatural worlds.</term>, into a second **Moses** and **Aaron** <note>In esoteric traditions, Moses and Aaron were viewed not just as biblical leaders, but as supreme magicians who commanded the elements and spoke directly with the divine.</note>, and that one receives all the secrets of nature and supreme authority over angels, devils, and men. The **Philosopher's Stone** <term>Stein der Weisen; the legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the Elixir of Life.</term> is the least of what these demigods possess.

# The Oath

I, [Name], promise freely and with careful consideration:

1. To worship the eternal, almighty **Jehovah** <note>A name for God frequently used in 18th-century Masonic rites to emphasize the "Great Architect" in a way that bridged different religious traditions.</note> in spirit and in truth for as long as I live.

# Remarks.

1. After the <term>Reception <note>The formal initiation or admission ceremony into a new degree of the Order.</note></term>, the <term>W. O. V. <note>Abbreviation for *Wohl-Ehrwürdiger Ober-Vorsteher*, meaning "Worshipful Presiding Officer."</note></term> shall read to the newly admitted <term>Brother <note>Abbreviated as "B." in the original.</note></term> something from the beginning of the instruction for the <term>Theoretical Brothers <note>The "Theorist" or "Theoretical Brother" was a specific rank within the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross, focused on studying the laws of nature and alchemy.</note></term>. After the Brother Secretary (<term>Secret.</term>) has read the laws to the Theorist, and the latter has promised to fulfill them by laying his hands upon the <term>Gospel of St. John <note>The Gospel of John is a foundational text in many esoteric and Masonic traditions, often used as the sacred book for taking oaths.</note></term>, the assembly is ended with the usual closing questions. However, should a banquet be held after the Reception, it should be conducted with all seriousness and decorum.

2. The W. O. V. may arrange the following instruction according to his will and divide every paragraph <note>original: "Paragraphum" (Latin-influenced German).</note> as he finds it necessary; he may also summarize it more briefly if the benefit of his Brothers requires it. For the intentions of the <term>Superiors <note>The "Superiors" (*Obern*) refers to the high-ranking, often hidden leaders of the secret society's hierarchy.</note></term> are directed only toward providing those eager for knowledge—

# Laws
## for the highly sublime Assembly of the Theoretical Brothers. <note>The "Theoretical Brothers" (Theoretische Brüder) represented the second degree in the 1777/1781 reorganization of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross, focusing on the study of nature and alchemy.</note>

->§. 1.<-

Since the true happiness of man consists in the striving for virtue and wisdom, we have wished to exhort the philosophical Brothers that, after diligent and industrious care for their worldly affairs, they [apply themselves] <note>The text cuts off here, continuing on the next page; the catchword "Folge" indicates the next word relates to "following" or "pursuing".</note> to

### § 2.
### Concerning Religion.

Every <term>theoretical brother<note>A "theoretical brother" refers to a specific rank or grade of initiate within the Order of the Gold and Rosy Cross, an 18th-century fraternal organization focused on alchemy and Christian mysticism.</note></term> must be devoted to a certain religion and follow it diligently and zealously, as is fitting for the duties of an honest man. Furthermore, everyone is free to give their approval to those opinions which they consider the most reasonable and beneficial, provided they are only such opinions that make a person pious, fair, honest, kind-hearted, and helpful toward their neighbor. Otherwise, it is sufficient if he is of Christian descent and faith, regardless of what lands he may otherwise come from.

### § 3.
### Concerning the Submission Owed to the Authorities.

Every member of these societies must be a loyal subject of his country

### §. 5.

### Concerning the Order which the Brothers Shall Maintain in the Assembly.

In the context of 18th-century secret societies, this refers specifically to a "thirst for knowledge" regarding the mysteries of nature, alchemy, and the divine.</note></term> brothers; consequently, the <term>Venerable S. B.<note>Original: "Ehrw. O. ## Note.

1. The first <term>sign</term> <note>original: "Zeichen." In initiatory societies, signs are physical gestures used for recognition and to convey symbolic truths.</note> consists of raising the first three fingers of the right hand <note>The text likely implies raising them toward heaven, though the specific object is omitted in this transcription.</note>, as a sign that we believe in one single, great, almighty God who fills all things in every length, breadth, and depth.

2. We most humbly beseech you, give us also the nourishment of souls, which is your holy Word and Spirit, so that we may be provided for both physically and spiritually. We wish to praise you forever and give honor to you alone, because you alone are the Eternal and the only Mighty One, Amen!

3. After the prayer, the Brothers remain standing, and the <term>Brother Master of Ceremonies</term> <note>original: "Br. Cerem. M." (Bruder Ceremonien-Meister). After he has drunk a little wine, he says: May God bless this wine for us. He then passes the chalice around and says: May it be for us a reminder of the covenant of unbreakable fidelity and love toward our Brothers.

4. Thereupon the Brothers sit down and eat, but observe silence <note>original: "Stillschweigen." Ritual silence was often used to encourage internal meditation on the spiritual significance of the communal meal.</note>.

# Various Questions
## for the Instruction of the Theorists. <note>The "Theoricus" or Theorist was the second rank or degree in the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross, focusing on the theoretical foundations of alchemy and nature.</note>

Q. From what did all things arise?
A. Out of the **Chaos**. <note>In this tradition, Chaos is not mere disorder, but the *materia prima* or "First Matter"—the raw, undifferentiated substance used by God to create the universe.</note>
Q. What was the **Chaos**?
A. It was a dark, formless mass, which consisted of <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: ▽</note> and <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: △</note>, and is indicated by the Hebrew word <term>the heavens</term> <note>original: "השמים" (ha-shamayim). In Rosicrucian linguistics, this word was often divided into "Esh" (fire) and "Mayim" (water).</note>; from this mass, through the almighty Word of God, all things [arose...]

things of this world were created. This was the **Materia** <note>original: *Materia*. In alchemy, this refers to the *Materia Prima* or "First Matter"—the raw, undifferentiated substance that contains the potential for all physical forms.</note> in which all forms and shapes were preserved in their power, and subsequently revealed through the will of the **Creator**.

Q. How did this come to pass?

A. The **Spirit of the Lord** hovered upon the water, Genesis 1, and through his life-giving power, he animated the **Chaos**, separated the light from the darkness, filled the void, illuminated the dark, set apart the mingled elements from one another, ripened the raw with his divine warmth, impregnated the infertile, and brought order to the disordered.

Q. What arose from the separation of the **Chaos**?

A. The angelic, the heavenly, and the elementary world.

Q. What is the **heavenly world**?

A. The seven **planets** and all the stars of the **firmament**. <note>In the traditional cosmology used by this Order, the "seven planets" refer to the Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note>

Q. What is the **Sun**?

A. It is the most pure and perfect body, which has been drawn from the **Light**, and is therefore capable of receiving <note>original: *per media zu concipiren* — to conceive or take in through intervening mediums.</note> the created Light as it flows out from its **center**, <note>original: *Centro*</note> and of communicating it to the remaining stars and things.

Q. What is the **Moon**?

A. The **Moon** is a planet which has received its body from the purest part of the **abyss**, <note>original: *Abgrundes*. In alchemy and Rosicrucianism, the "abyss" often refers to the dark, watery potential of the primordial Chaos from which the world was formed.</note> and receives its light from the **Sun**; it then gives that light a physical form <note>original: *corporistret* — to corporize, or to give a spiritual substance a more material body.</note> with its own essence, and subsequently distributes it in turn to the lower things.

Q. From where do the other **planets** receive their light?

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the Rosicrucian catechism, answering the question posed at the end of the previous page regarding the source of light for the planets. It transitions from the "heavenly world" (the stars and planets) to the "elementary world" (the material realm).</meta>

A. The remaining **planets** and **stars** all obtain their light from the **Sun**, as the general receiver of the created light. Conversely, the other stars constantly reflect the light back to the **Sun** from the essence of their bodies, each according to its order; whereby the **Sun** is maintained in its power and splendor, casts forth its rays, fills the air with them, and through the air sends them to the <term>sublunar</term> <note>sublunar: literally "under the moon," a term used in traditional cosmology to describe the Earthly realm of the four elements, which is subject to change and decay, unlike the eternal and "perfect" celestial spheres.</note> bodies.

Q. What is the **elementary world**?

A. The solid body which the **Creator**, during the separation of <term>Chaos</term>, <note>original: *Chaos*. In alchemical and Rosicrucian thought, this refers to the *materia prima* or the primal, unformed state of matter before God's word imposed order upon it.</note> separated as the coarsest part from the purest part.

Q. Was this world-structure then immediately placed into its perfect state?

A. No! Rather, **God** first created the **Spirit** out of His powerful **Word**.

Q. Why the **Spirit** first?

A. Because the **Spirit**, both in the **Godhead** and in the creatures, is the fundamental essence and <term>primary matter</term> <note>original: *Urstof*. This refers to the primordial substance or "first material" from which all subsequent physical and spiritual forms are derived.</note> of all things.

<summary>This page explains the reciprocal exchange of light between the Sun and other celestial bodies, and defines the "elementary world" as the result of the separation of Chaos. It emphasizes that the Spirit was the first creation and serves as the fundamental substance of all existence.</summary>
<keywords>Sun, light, sublunar, elementary world, Chaos, Spirit, primary matter, cosmology, creation</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the Rosicrucian catechism. Having established that the Spirit is the primary matter of all things, the text now describes how God organized this Spirit to create the physical universe through the principles of contraction and expansion. Q. What did **God** ordain with this **Spirit** at the foundation of this world?

A. The **Almighty** let this **Spirit**, proceeding from Him, seek the **center** <note>original: *Mittelpunct*. In Hermetic thought, the center is the point of origin from which all creation radiates.</note> of the **world-structure**, establish itself there, and <term>contract</term> <note>original: *zusammenziehen*. Contraction or "compression" was considered a vital alchemical process where spirit densifies into matter.</note> itself; in which **center** it could give birth to yet more beings from itself, and then, from the **center** of its dominion, expand into a proper <term>circumspheric</term> <note>original: *circumsphärische*. This refers to the spherical field of influence surrounding a central point, describing how the universe occupies a three-dimensional volume.</note> distance.

Q. Was this **central contraction** necessary?

A. Certainly; for we see with our own eyes that without **contraction**, even now, no thing—neither animal, nor herb, nor metal—can come into being, and consequently this **world-structure** would also not have come to light.

Q. Were other beings then necessary for this **world-structure**?

A. Yes! for a simple, thin **Spirit** could not have constituted the multiplicity and diversity of this **world-structure**; therefore—

—therefore the universal **Spirit** had to create even more beings for the diversity of this world through **central contraction**.

**Q. What other kind of being did the Spirit create through the aforementioned contraction?**
**A.** A second kind of its essence, which we call the **Soul**.

**Q. What is this Soul called by the Solomonic sage?** <note>Referring to King Solomon, who in the Western Mystery Tradition was considered the archetype of the wise natural philosopher and magus.</note>
**A. Nephesch** <note>original Hebrew: נֶפֶשׁ. While linguistically this term usually means "breath" or "living being," the text here provides a specific Hermetic interpretation.</note>, which means a **detached branch**, because the **Soul** was taken from the universal **World-Spirit**.

**Q. How did this branching off occur?**
**A.** In the contracting movement, the **Spirit**—through the compression of its essence—took a portion of its essence away from itself and consequently contracted it somewhat more densely than its own essence was. This essence, branching off or separated from the **Spirit**, consequently also received a different power and movement.

**Q. Was the Soul, with its contracting power, sufficient to represent the great world-structure?** <note>original: *Weltbau*. This term suggests the universe is a deliberate architectural construction or "building."</note>

<summary>This page describes the second stage of creation, where the universal Spirit contracts a portion of itself to create the Soul (Nephesch). This process of densification allows for the diversity of the physical world to begin taking shape.</summary>
<keywords>Soul, Nephesch, Spirit, world-structure, central contraction, Solomon, Hermetic cosmogony</keywords>

<meta>catchword: Der; this page continues a philosophical dialogue regarding the hierarchy of existence—Spirit, Soul, and Body—and transitions into the formal closing of a meeting.</meta>

A. No; for although the aforementioned soul received a denser essence than the spirit itself, it was still far too spiritual, invisible, and simple. Therefore, it was not yet sufficient for the diversity of this great edifice of the world <note>The "edifice of the world" (Weltgebäude) is a common Enlightenment-era term for the organized structure of the universe or cosmos.</note>, and consequently, a third essence was necessary.

Q. <note>original: "Fr." for "Frage" (Question)</note> What kind of essence was this?

A. A body—that is, the earth—as the coarsest part separated from the purest light so that it might multiply itself, as the three kingdoms of nature <note>In historical natural philosophy, the "three kingdoms" refer to the animal, vegetable, and mineral classifications of the natural world.</note> demonstrate daily. The soul is therefore only a transformed essence of the spirit, and the body a transformed and denser essence of the soul.

<meta>This page continues the ritual catechism of the Theoretical degree within the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross. Having established the identity of the "Theoretical Philosopher" on the previous page, the dialogue now shifts to the fundamental definition of Nature and the moral requirements for those who wish to study her secrets.</meta>

The W. G. V. <term>W.G.V.: Abbreviation for *Würdiger Groß-Vorsteher* (Worthy Grand Presiding Officer).</term> What is Nature?

A. Nature is an invisible, volatile <note>original: *flüchtiger*. In an alchemical context, "volatile" refers to a spirit or substance that is subtle, active, and capable of transformation or movement, as opposed to being "fixed" or solid.</note> spirit, which nevertheless works visibly in physical bodies, and has its seat in the divine will.

The G. V. Through what have the Freemasons symbolized Nature?

A. Through the <image-desc>hexagram with a central dot</image-desc> which represents the divine breath, the universal and central <image-desc>upward-pointing triangle symbol</image-desc> <note>The upward-pointing triangle is the alchemical symbol for Fire. Here it represents the "Central Fire" or *Archeus*, the hidden spiritual heat that Rosicrucians believed was the life-force of the universe.</note> which creates and animates all things.

Fr. <note>Abbreviation for *Frage* (Question).</note> What qualities must the researchers of Nature possess?

A. The researchers of Nature must be constituted like Nature itself, namely: truthful, simple <note>original: *einfältig*. In the 18th century, this term carried a positive connotation of being sincere, pure-hearted, and without guile.</note>, patient, steadfast, pious and God-fearing, kind-hearted and helpful toward their neighbor. All these qualities are instilled in the brothers of the lower degrees <note>original: *untern Stuffe*. This refers to the three basic degrees of Blue Lodge Masonry—Entered Apprentice, Fellow Craft, and Master Mason—which served as the foundation for the higher Rosicrucian grades.</note> of Masonry upon their admission.

Fr. Into how many regions is Nature divided?

->A. In<-

<meta>This page continues the ritual catechism of the Theoretical degree of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross. The previous page discussed the character of the researcher; this page focuses on the structure of Nature and concludes the opening phase of the lodge meeting.</meta>

A. Into 4 regions: the warm, cold, dry, and moist, which are the elementary qualities <note>original: *elementarische Eigenschaften*. These refer to the Aristotelian qualities that combine to form the four classical elements: Fire (warm/dry), Air (warm/moist), Water (cold/moist), and Earth (cold/dry).</note> through which all things are worked.

Q. <note>original: *Fr.*, abbreviation for *Frage*.</note> Into what does Nature transform itself?
A. It transforms itself into male and female, and is compared to Mercury <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note>.

Q. How many beginnings <note>original: *Anfänge*. In alchemical theory, these are the fundamental principles or "origins" of all matter.</note> does Nature have?
A. It has 3 beginnings, namely Salt <note>represented by the symbol 🜔</note>, Sulfur <note>represented by the symbol 🜍</note>, and Mercury <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note>, by means of which all things are brought forth, nourished, and maintained. <note>These three—Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—are known as the *Tria Prima*, a concept popularized by Paracelsus to describe the spiritual and physical composition of all substances.</note>

Q. How many kingdoms of Nature are there?
A. Three: the mineral, vegetable, and animal.

Q. When should a philosopher <note>original: *Philosoph*. In this context, it refers specifically to an "alchemical philosopher" or seeker of the Stone.</note> begin his work?
A. At the break of day, thereby demonstrating his diligence and vigilance.

The W. S. O. <term>W.S.O.: Abbreviation for *Würdiger Ober-Beaufseher* (Worthy Senior Overseer), a presiding officer who assists the Master of the Lodge.</term> strikes 7 blows with the gavel upon the altar and speaks: "Let the assembly hereby be opened."

# Conclusion
## of the Meeting.

The W. S. O. <term>W.S.O.: Abbreviation for *Würdiger Ober-Beaufseher* (Worthy Senior Overseer), the officer who assisted in presiding over the assembly.</term> speaks: Worthy Brothers! Does anyone have anything to bring forward for the good of the assembly?

If no notice is given, the W. S. O. asks the Br. Secretary.

Q. <note>original: *Fr.*, abbreviation for *Frage* (Question).</note> When does a philosopher <note>In the context of this Order, a "philosopher" is not merely an academic, but a "Spiritual Chemist" or alchemist seeking the hidden truths of God and Nature.</note> rest?

A. When the work <note>original: *die Arbeit*. This refers to the "Great Work" (*Magnum Opus*) of alchemical and spiritual transformation.</note> has attained perfection.

Q. What time is it at the end of the work?

A. High noon <note>original: *Voll Mittag*. In Masonic and Rosicrucian symbolism, "Noon" represents the peak of enlightenment and the time when the "sun" of wisdom is at its zenith.</note>; that is the moment when the sun is in its greatest strength, and the moon is in its full splendor.

Q. What age does a seeking philosopher have?

A. From the moment of his investigations until that of his discovery, he does not age. <note>This likely alludes to the Rosicrucian concept of the "Elixir of Life" or the idea that the soul, once engaged in the pursuit of divine truth, transcends the linear decay of the physical body.</note>

<meta>catchword: Q.</meta>

<summary>This page details the ritual closing of a Rosicrucian meeting, using a question-and-answer format to explain the symbolic "High Noon" of spiritual completion and the timeless nature of the seeker.</summary>
<keywords>Closing ritual, Worthy Senior Overseer, alchemical work, High Noon, spiritual perfection, symbolic age, Golden and Rosy Cross</keywords>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page continues the closing ritual of the Theoretical degree of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross. It features the exchange of the order's secret "word," the formal closing of the lodge by the presiding officer, and the beginning of a liturgical prayer.</meta>

Q. <note>original: *Fr.*, abbreviation for *Frage* (Question).</note> Tell me the word of Magnesia? <note>In alchemy, "Magnesia" does not refer to the modern chemical element, but rather the "Prime Matter"—the raw, chaotic substance required to begin the Great Work of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

A. It is contained in these words: *Visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying, you will find the hidden stone, the true medicine (that is Vitriol).* <note>original Latin: *Visita interiora terrae ratificando, invenies occultum lapidem, veram medicinam (id est Vitriolum)*. This is the famous alchemical acronym "V.I.T.R.I.O.L." It serves as a mnemonic for the meditative process of looking inward to find spiritual truth.</note> Will you pass to me the sign, the touch, and the word of the Philosophers?

One Brother gives it to the other, and the last two pass it to the W. O. V. <term>W.O.V.: Abbreviation for *Würdiger Ober-Vorsteher* (Worthy Senior Superior), the primary officer presiding over the lodge.</term> on both sides.

The W. O. V. speaks: The assembly is hereby closed, and strikes 7 times upon the altar. Following this, the prayer after the assembly is spoken.

## **Instruction of the Theoretical Brothers.**

<note>The "Theoretical Brothers" (Theoretische Brüder) refers to the second degree of the Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross, a 18th-century Masonic-Rosicrucian society focused on alchemy and Christian mysticism.</note>

**T**he W. O. B. <note>Likely an abbreviation for "Würdigster Ober-Bruder" (Most Worthy Senior Brother), the presiding officer of the lodge.</note> speaks:

Since we are gathered here to instruct ourselves in Nature, and through her to better know God our Creator and ourselves, I ask: what is God?

->A Brother speaks:<-

A reverent silence befits our weak reason better than wishing to fathom God in His essence; for as finite creatures, we can neither speak nor think of this infinite Good, as a part cannot comprehend the whole. However, the Solomonic philosophy <note>Refers to a tradition of esoteric wisdom attributed to King Solomon, often involving the study of the hidden forces of nature and divine architecture.</note> teaches us that God is an eternal, immeasurable, invisible Spirit, who reigns in an uncreated light, from whence

...in which he has revealed to us his incomprehensible wisdom and omnipotence through the creation of the world. Through his holy Word, the invisible became visible, and his divine breath animates all of nature.

**Q.** What is nature?
**A.** Nature is an invisible spirit, which nevertheless works visibly within bodies, and has its seat in the divine will.

**Q.** From what did nature originate?
**A.** When we contemplate the created works of God in general, we find that **Chaos** <term>In Rosicrucian and alchemical thought, Chaos is not "disorder," but the "Massa Confusa"—the primal, undifferentiated matter containing all the potential of the universe.</term> was and still is the fundamental beginning of all created things.

**Q.** What is Chaos?
->§. 1.<-
**A.** Chaos was a mixed mass <note>original: *Klumpen*, meaning a lump or clod. In this context, it refers to the primal "First Matter."</note>, which was moved by water, and this [water] was animated by fire; out of this mass, through the almighty and eternal Word of God, all things of this world were created and brought forth. This

<summary>This page defines nature as an invisible spirit seated in the divine will and introduces "Chaos" as the primal substance of creation. It explains that the world was formed from this mass through the interaction of water, fire, and the divine Word.</summary>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>The previous page appears to have been discussing the origins of the universe or the primordial state of creation; this page continues that description, beginning with the word "was," likely referring to the "Chaos" or "First Matter" mentioned in the following lines. The catchword from the previous page was "Hin-", suggesting a word like "Hingegen" (on the other hand) or "Hinführo" (henceforth) might have bridged the pages, though the current page starts directly with the description of Matter.</meta>

...was the Matter <term>Materia: the fundamental, unformed substance from which all things in the universe are created</term> in which all forms and shapes were contained in potential <note>original Latin: *potentialiter*</note>, and subsequently through the will of the eternal God were revealed and put into action. This formless lump—namely, the Chaos—was watery, and is signified by the little Hebrew word *Shamayim* <note>Hebrew: שמים (Shamayim), meaning 'heavens.' Alchemists often interpreted this word as a compound of *Esh* (fire) and *Mayim* (water)</note>, which means fire (△) and water (▽) and Matter; this material water (▽) was divided by God into the upper, middle, and lower parts. 

The upper part is perfectly illuminated and very subtle <note>In this context, 'subtle' refers to matter that is refined, ethereal, and spiritual rather than dense and physical</note> compared to all others; the lowest part, on the other hand, is perfectly dark, impure, coarse, and thick. The middle part has something of both and is mixed with the upper and the lower. 

The lower part, or the third class, nevertheless comprises and contains within itself the properties, virtues, powers, and essences of all beings from the creatures of the first class, though with this distinction: that what the lower creatures are in their manifest form, the upper creatures are in a potential power and a hidden essence.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical creation of the world from a primordial, watery "Chaos," which God divided into three distinct realms: the subtle upper, the coarse lower, and the mixed middle. It explains the relationship between spiritual potential in the upper realms and physical manifestation in the lower.</summary>

<keywords>Matter, Chaos, Shamayim, alchemy, creation, elements, potentiality, subtle matter, cosmology</keywords>

### § 2. Concerning the Elements in General.

An element is a body separated from the <term>Chaos</term> <note>In alchemy, "Chaos" refers to the "Materia Prima" or the raw, unformed substance from which God created the world</note>, in and through which elemental things exist; or it is the beginning of a thing, just as a letter is the beginning of a syllable.

The science and doctrine of the elements is of the highest importance and necessity; for it is the key to the sacred mysteries of Nature, without which everything remains locked away. The elements have a close relationship and communion with one another and easily change from one into another, as we shall show hereafter.

§. 3.

## On the Individual Elements, and Specifically of Fire <note>Original: △, the alchemical symbol for Fire.</note>

1. Fire and Air <note>Original: 🜁, the alchemical symbol for Air.</note> are the upper elements. Fire, as the first, precedes all others because of its purity, subtlety <note>German: *Dünnigkeit*; in alchemical philosophy, this refers to a substance being refined, non-dense, and able to permeate other matter.</note>, and perfection, which it attains from simplicity <term>simplicity <note>German: *Simplicität*; the state of being unmixed or undivided, which was considered a divine attribute.</note></term>, and it has therefore become nobler and mightier than the others. The universal **World Spirit** <term>World Spirit <note>German: *Weltgeist* (Anima Mundi); a vital spiritual force thought by alchemists to permeate all physical matter.</note></term> has its seat in this element and imparts wondrous powers to the element of Fire.

2. Air, because it is not as pure as Fire, can never thoroughly penetrate the element of Fire, nor unite itself entirely with Fire, unless it has first been cleansed of its impurity.

3. Elemental Fire only acts when it is concentrated and driven together; this occurs when its rays are strengthened and their emanations are cast powerfully from it.

4. When the almighty **Creator** [created] the elements (Genesis 1:10) and all elemented <insert>things</insert>

### §. 4. Of

...a likeness and image of the upper world; and just as in this lower world the <term>Air</term> <note>symbol: 🜁</note> remains above the <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: ∇</note>, and above the Air the <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: △</note> resides, so also in the <term>angelic world</term> <note>German: *englische Welt*. In this period, "englisch" typically refers to angels (*Engel*), the inhabitants of the celestial hierarchy, rather than the English nation</note> the upper heavenly Air is above the super-celestial Water; and above both of these is elevated the most pure Fire: within this is the incomprehensible, immeasurable light of the most holy majesty of God.

15. Let no one complain against us that we do not speak more of this high matter; we can say nothing of it if we do not wish to act against God and the Holy Scripture. However, a secret key is to be found which opens the gates of these <term>secrets of nature</term> <note>German: *Naturgeheimnisse*; refers to the hidden laws of the universe that alchemists sought to uncover</note>.

16. This key lies in a common and very well-known body, despicable in the eyes of the common people, but held very high and precious in the eyes of those who understand nature and wisdom <note>This is a classic alchemical reference to the *Materia Prima* (First Matter), which philosophers claimed was a humble, overlooked substance that served as the foundation for the Philosopher's Stone</note>.

### §. 4.
## On the Air.

1. The <term>Air</term> <note>symbol: 🜁</note> is a very subtle, translucent, invisible, and light element; it is the bond between the upper and the lower things; a dwelling-place for the <term>celestial signs of air</term> <note>The zodiac signs of Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius</note> and <term>meteors</term> <note>In the 17th and 18th centuries, "meteors" referred to all atmospheric phenomena, including clouds, wind, rain, and lightning</note>. Nothing in the world can do without this element, for from it all creatures draw nourishment and life; it strengthens their <term>generative sap</term> <note>German: *Erzeugungssaft*; the vital fluids responsible for growth and reproduction</note> and nourishes the <term>vital spirits</term> <note>German: *Lebensgeister*; the subtle substances then believed to circulate through the body to maintain life and movement</note>. Indeed, nothing would be born into this world if the Air did not penetrate everything and draw the increasing power of nourishment into itself. The Air, says <term>Sendivogius</term> <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636) was a renowned Polish alchemist who famously wrote about a "hidden food of life" found in the air, which many modern historians believe was a precursor to the discovery of oxygen</note>, has within it a hidden food of life, whose coagulated <note>Original text includes a symbol for spirit or salt, resembling a horizontal line with a squiggle above</note> essence is better than the whole inhabited world.

2. This element is purer than the <term>Earth</term> <note>symbol: 🜃</note>, but not as pure as the Heavens or the <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: 🜂</note>; it partakes in the purity of the upper element and is richly endowed with the universal <term>World Spirit</term> <note>Latin: *Spiritus Mundi*; a hypothetical substance that alchemists believed permeated the universe and acted as the medium for divine influence and life</note>.

<meta>catchword: §. 5.</meta>

## §. 5
# On the Water.

1. The <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note> and the <term>Earth</term> <note>symbol: 🜃</note> are the lower elements. The <term>elevation</term> <note>In alchemy, "elevation" refers to the process of refining a substance or raising its spiritual "vibrancy," often through distillation or circulation</note> of both depends on the excellence of the upper elements <note>Air and Fire</note>, and it is especially required for the attainment of their perfection that they be elevated and enriched many times by the upper powers; it is required, I say, that the <term>Earth</term> <note>symbol: 🜃</note> be frequently elevated by means of the <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note>, so that the <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: 🜂</note>, which is located in the innermost part of the earth, may appear and be made manifest and active. The <term>Water</term> <note>symbol: 🜄</note> never returns empty to the earth without bringing some new powers with it and imparting them to the <term>Earth</term> <note>symbol: 🜃</note>.

### §. 6.
## On the Earth.

1. This is the lowest element: thick, impure, dry, and the dwelling of animals, plants, metals, and minerals. It contains an innumerable multitude of seeds <note>The alchemical concept that all things grow from "seeds" or spiritual sparks planted in matter</note> within itself and is not as simple as the other three elements, for which the <term>Earth</term> <note>original symbol: 🜃</note> is, as it were, a collection box and dwelling.

2. The <term>Earth</term> <note>original symbol: 🜃</note> is a fixed <note>The term "fixed" refers to matter that remains stable in fire and does not evaporate</note> and <term>fire-persistent</term> <note>original: *🜂 beständiger*; indicating the Earth's ability to withstand the element of Fire (🜂) without being consumed</note> body, which retains the impressions of the upper influences <note>likely referring to astrological or celestial "rays" that shape the material world</note> better than all other elements. <term>Water</term> <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> and <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: 🜂</note> do not retain such influences as easily; for these influences penetrate through to the innermost center of the Earth and emerge from there frequently to its outermost surface.

### §. 9.
## On Salt.

1. <term>Salt (🜔)</term> is a fixed substance and a fundamental beginning <note>original: *Grundanfang*</note> of things. It is likened to the air-earth <note>This likely refers to the "Philosophical Earth," a substance that is solid yet possesses spiritual or "aerial" qualities</note>; it nourishes <term>Sulfur (🜂)</term> and <term>Mercury (☿)</term>, which produce their effects within it. This continues until the Sulfur and Mercury have made the Salt volatile <note>Capable of being evaporated or turned into spirit</note> like themselves, and have brought it to their same level of perfection. The Salt, on the other hand, holds and coagulates <note>solidifies</note> the two of them, also imparting to them a fixed and dry nature; it binds together everything that is fluid. When the Salt has been dissolved into a suitable liquor <note>original: *Liquorem*; a liquid solvent</note>, it then also helps to dissolve all dense bodies, just as, on the other hand, its fixed nature consolidates all open <note>porous or loose</note> bodies. When it has been dissolved with Sulfur and Mercury, its increasing power then gains strength, and its potential is put into action.

### §. 13.
## Concerning Generation.

1. The generation <term>Gebährung|the process of bringing forth or birth</term> of every body occurs in and with its own seed within its own matrix <term>Gebährmutter|literally "womb," but used alchemically to mean the specific vessel or environment where a substance matures</term>; and if the seed is not perfect, and the matrix is not pure and natural, no generation can take place. The seed from animals requires an animal matrix; from vegetables, a vegetable matrix; and from minerals, a mineral matrix. This is well worth noting, so that one does not fall into the common errors which are daily committed by many in the alchemical art <note>original: $\Delta$ *Kunst*; the triangle symbol for fire represents the "Art of Fire," a traditional name for chemistry and alchemy.</note>. A good and species-appropriate matrix is one which is properly suited to the seed of its own kind; and how can it fail that a seed should bring forth its like, when it [acts] according to nature <note>The page ends mid-sentence; the catchword "gemäß" indicates the text continues as "according to nature."</note>

### §. 14.
## On Preservation.

The preservation of natural things occurs by the same power as their birth <term>Gebährung — the process of generation or bringing something into being</term>, yet with this distinction: preservation is achieved through the acceptance and consumption of external materials. Within the nourishment consumed, two types of matter are found: one which is accepted by nature as suitable and is united and incorporated <note>original: *incorporiret*; a term used in alchemy and natural philosophy to describe how one substance becomes part of the body of another.</note> with it, while the other, being contrary to nature, is discarded.

2. The sustaining power <term>Nahrungskraft — the vital energy or "nutritive force" that allows a living thing to extract life from food</term> which performs this preservation is both spiritual and material. The first is invisible and intangible within the food; the other, however, is visible and tangible, though not as subtle or penetrating as the former. This pure essence is nothing other than the universal world-spirit <term>Weltgeist — also known as the *Spiritus Mundi*, this was believed to be a subtle, divine breath or substance that permeated all of creation and gave life to matter</term> dwelling within it, which, like a structure, connects the invisible with

That which can make food most powerful and perfect is simplicity—or the subtle, simple essence <note>original: *Wessen* (Wesen)</note> found within the composition, especially when such a composition is not put together from many different things. ### §. 13.
### On Destruction.

1. The destruction of elemental things <term>elementirten Dinge — physical matter composed of the four elements (earth, air, fire, water)</term> commonly occurs through the opposite of a thing, when one quality dominates and overcomes the other. This is accomplished either through dissolution <term>Dissolution — the process of breaking down a solid into a liquid or spiritual state</term> or coagulation <term>Coagulation — the process of thickening or solidifying a substance</term>. The coarser dissolving occurs through grinding, breaking, and the like; the more subtle, however, occurs through a gentle and nature-conforming resolution of the bodies, through which they are placed into a constant and perfect nature; then the coagulation of the new body causes a destruction of the old, and let this be enough said regarding the three occurrences <note>original: *Zufällen* — here referring to the three modes of change discussed in the text: generation, renewal, and destruction</note> of nature; we must further consider

## §. 16.
# The Effect of the Upper Celestial Bodies.

1. From the upper celestial bodies <term>obern Gestirnen — the stars, planets, and the firmament</term> come the influences for the propagation <term>Fortpflanzung — the reproduction or multiplication of forms</term> of the various shells <note>original: *Gehäuse* — referring to the physical bodies or "containers" that house the spirit of a thing</note> in the three kingdoms <note>the animal, vegetable, and mineral kingdoms</note>, according to the properties of each. The light of every body among the upper celestial entities does not rest, but always works and strives to pull toward and upon itself the lower light, which is found in the various seed-bodies <term>Saamenkörpern — seeds or the fundamental germinal points of matter</term>; in like manner, the lower light-essence <term>Lichtessenz — the internal spiritual spark or "inner sun" of matter</term> within the seed works to pull the upper [light] down to itself, and through this upward and downward pulling of the upper and lower light, the movement and growth of all natural things occurs. This influence of the stars <term>Gestirnes</term> is a subtle body <note>a fine, ethereal substance that mediates between spirit and matter</note>, which is capable of communicating itself to the lower things by means of rays. When these influences are simple, that is, from an individual star, then their effect <term>Würkung</term> is also only simple; but the influence <term>Influenz</term> mingled with many rays works differently in the lower bodies, in that it either accelerates their action,

### §. 19.
## On the Generation of Metals.

This occurs in the following manner: the universal World Spirit <note>The *spiritus mundi*, a subtle substance believed by alchemists to permeate the universe and act as the "glue" between spirit and matter.</note> mingles with the ▽ <gloss>Water</gloss> and the 🜄 <gloss>Earth</gloss>, from which is formed a greasy vapor <note>original: "Schmierter Broden"</note>; this distills into the center of the 🜄 <gloss>Earth</gloss>, and then steams upward again. This refers to the "root essence" or the inner power of the substance.</note> hidden within the ☿ <gloss>Mercury</gloss> is elevated and becomes dominant; then the ☿ <gloss>Mercury</gloss> is transformed into a <note>Symbol: Mercury with a cross top instead of horns, representing a partially fixed or "ennobled" mercury</note> according to the nature of the pure or impure 🜍 <gloss>Sulfur</gloss> it has encountered. For an impure matrix <term>Matrise — the subterranean "womb" or mineral environment where metals grow</term> and impure 🜍 <gloss>Sulfur</gloss> hinder the adopted ☿ <gloss>Mercury</gloss>, so that it cannot reach a perfect <note>Symbol: Cross over a circle with a flourish, signifying a perfected or "fixed" metallic state, such as gold</note> state.

The **setting back** <note>original: "Zurückstellung"; likely referring to the internal positioning or the restraint of the lower soul's faculties to allow higher functions to emerge.</note> of this animal soul excites an **imagination** <term>imagination</term> <note>original: "Imagination oder Einbildung"; in this historical context, imagination was seen as a potent force that could shape the physical body and the will, rather than just "make-believe."</note>, and then causes a desire or a will. This is carried out through the movement of the bodily parts, which are associated with the dwelling-place of the **sensitive soul** <term>sensitive soul</term> <note>the part of the soul responsible for sensory perception and physical instincts.</note> of perfect or imperfect **reason** <note>original: "Vernunft"</note>, and depend upon them. From this it follows that some animals show more or less perfection in their works, according to the way their **instruments** <note>original: "Werkzeuge"; the physical organs, limbs, or biological structures of a living being.</note> are proportioned and shaped.

## Man.

1. This is the most perfect **creature** <note>original: "Creatur"</note> of all; therefore, his body is also made and prepared in the most excellent way, because this is necessarily required for his magnificent functions.

## §. 29.

## On the Diseases of Humans.
### Firstly, regarding the diseases of the body.

1. A disease is actually nothing other than a destruction and corruption of the **natural balsam** <term>Balsam; in early modern medicine, this refers to the "radical moisture" or vital life-force believed to preserve the body from decay</term> in a part of the human body. When one disease is joined by another, it is called a **complication** <note>original: "Zufall"; in this period, it referred to a secondary symptom or an incidental ailment occurring alongside a primary disease</note>.

2. External infirmities and accidents of man occur through a **dissolution** <note>original: "Auflösung"; referring to the "solution of continuity," a medical term for when tissues that should be connected are separated by injury</note> of the composite parts; namely, when an external part of the body is injured by a wound, ulcer, dislocation, fracture, or bite. Ulcers are caused by various kinds of decay, corrosive moisture, or inflammation; types of these are cankers <note>original: "Aisten"; likely referring to aphthae or mouth ulcers</note>, abscesses, "the wolf" <term>Wolf; an archaic term for lupus or any corrosive skin condition that "eats" the flesh</term>, cancer, gangrene <note>original: "faule Fleisch," literally "rotten flesh"</note>, nasal ulcers, whitlow <term>Nägelschwang; a painful infection of the fingertip or nail bed</term>, smallpox, and the like. Dislocation of the limbs occurs when the bones are moved out of their joints or sockets. A fracture occurs if a limb is broken by a fall or similar. A rupture <note>original: "Riß"; literally a tear or rip</note> occurs in the membranes and causes, in the omentum, a scrotal hernia or rupture <note>original: "Karnaffel"; an early modern term for an inguinal hernia</note>; that is, when the intestines fall down into the scrotum.

1. **Indigestion** <term>Unverdauung</term> occurs in the human body when the nutritional juice <note>original: "Speiß- und Nahrungssaft"; referring to chyle, the milky fluid into which food is converted during digestion</note> or the blood has not been properly cooked and refined <note>Digestion was historically described as a "cooking" process (concoction) powered by the body's internal heat</note>. Also, the neglect of physical exercise contributes significantly to this, especially when the natural heat is not stimulated and exercised. Such indigestion then causes much inconvenience in the body. In the stomach, it causes a loathing of food or a disordered desire to eat earth, chalk, coal, and the like <note>A reference to pica, a condition often associated in period medical texts with digestive or nutritional imbalances</note>. If the slimy **crudities** <term>Cruditäten; undigested or "raw" matter that the body failed to process</term> attach themselves to the stomach and intestines and are heated up, then a **putrefaction** <note>original: "Faulung"</note> occurs, which [produces] worms... <note>Early modern medicine often held that intestinal worms were "spontaneously generated" from corrupt, rotting humors or undigested food</note>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on treating diseases of the humors, transitioning from the causes of paralysis and apoplexy on page 201 to the general cures for these ailments, followed by a new section on the dangers of internal blockages.</meta>

All such diseases are prevented and cured: firstly through external **exercise of the limbs**, secondly through a good and **fragrant fumigation** of the brain, thirdly with **warm and sulfurous air**, and finally with **proper order in food and drink** <note>This refers to "dietetics," one of the core pillars of early medicine focusing on lifestyle and nutrition</note>; beyond all this, special and well-prepared **specifics** <term>specifica; medicines or remedies specifically formulated to treat a particular organ or disease</term> must be applied for such illnesses and infirmities.

**4. Concerning Blockage.** <term>Verstopfung; in this context, the term refers to any internal obstruction or "stopping up" of the body's vessels and organs, not only the digestive tract</term> Such blockage likewise causes various infirmities; in the intestines, it causes **bowel-gout** <term>Darmgicht; a term for severe abdominal cramping or colic</term>; in the liver, it causes **dropsy** <term>Wassersucht; an accumulation of fluid in the body, known today as edema</term>, because the **nutritional juice** <term>Speisesaft; the liquid result of digestion, or chyme</term> cannot be properly "cooked" into blood, and therefore it is not **assimilated** <note>original: "memorificiret"; likely a variation of "membrificiret," meaning to be converted into the substance of the body's members</note>; in the gall <note>the gallbladder</note> it brings about **jaundice**; in the spleen, **black jaundice** <note>Often associated with "melancholy" or black bile</note>, because such blocked organs cannot function, and then their juices pour into the blood, which is then colored by them. In the kidneys and in the bladder, **blockage** causes **gravel and stones**, which obstruct the vessels and the urinary passages.

**5. In such cases, one must then cleanse the afflicted area and undertake the cure with a good **dietary regimen** <term>Speisordnung</term> and movement.

**6. For it is well known that inflammation is caused by movement, and it can likewise occur through blockage. For we see that watery and putrid things give off heat and eventually ignite, just as can be seen with hay that has been brought into the barn while wet <note>This refers to spontaneous combustion, a common historical analogy used to explain how "damp" or "corrupt" humors in the body could generate dangerous internal heat</note>. If the inflammation occurs internally...

Fever is either the ephemeral <note>original: "eintägige"; literally a "one-day" fever, usually brief and non-recurring</note>, the putrid <note>original: "faulende"</note>, or the hectic fever <term>Herzfieber; literally "heart-fever," a chronic, wasting fever that was believed to have moved from the fluids into the solid organs</term>. The first ignites the spirits <term>Spiritus; the vital forces believed to circulate through the body to maintain life</term>, the second the humors <term>Feuchtigkeiten; the bodily fluids</term>, and the third all parts of the body; therefore, the first is to be compared to a burning wind, the second to a burning water which warms the vessel into which it is poured, and the third to a warmed vessel which warms the water poured into it along with itself; for the hectic fever occupies all parts of the body with an unnatural heat, and gradually consumes them entirely. This fever is very similar to consumption <term>Schwindsucht; a historical term for wasting diseases like tuberculosis</term>. The putrid fever dominates the blood, the <term>humors</term> <note>original: "Humores"</note> or fluids, and heats the whole body.

The putrid fevers <note>original: "faulen Fieber"</note> are the most common and are of many different kinds. When the humors <term>Feuchtigkeit; the bodily fluids or humors, such as blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile</term> begin to rot within their vessels—especially those near the heart in the bile or liver—they are ignited by the spirit <term>Spiritus; the vital force believed to circulate through the body to maintain life</term> and are contested until the spirit either casts off the rot (which has been turned into a kind of soot) or is itself extinguished by it. Therefore, such fevers are frequently fatal and are called **continual fevers** <note>original: "stetswährende"; a fever that stays constant without breaks</note>. 

However, if the humors are located outside of their vessels—that is, in the veins <note>original: "Geädern"; generally refers to the vascular system</note> or other limbs—then an **intermittent fever** <term>unterlassenes Fieber; a fever that stops and starts at regular intervals, often associated with malaria</term> results. In this case, the spirit <term>Spiritus</term> only attacks such rot at certain times, and because this conflict is far removed from the heart, the spirit returns to its dwelling <note>original: "Wohnhauß"; the heart was traditionally viewed as the "house" or seat of the vital spirits</note> once the task is done. When the putrefying humor is spirituous <note>original: "spirituös"; here meaning light, airy, or volatile</note>, the spirit repeats this conflict immediately on the follow-

If the putrefying humor <note>original: "faulende Feuchtigkeit"</note> is the **yellow bile**, then this conflict occurs every three days, and it is the **three-day fever** <term>3tägige Fieber; historically known as a tertian fever</term>; if it is the **black bile**, then it is the **four-day fever** <term>4tägige Fieber; historically known as a quartan fever</term>. 

The reason for such unequal struggles between the vital spirit <note>original: "Lebensgeiſt"</note> and the putrid humor is that the watery humor immediately gathers again and thus gives the spirits <note>original: "Spiritibus"</note> work to do, but it is also divided all the more quickly by the vital spirits, which is why it occurs only daily. On the other hand, because the melancholic humor or the **black bile** is viscous and thick, it cannot replenish itself as quickly, nor can it be overcome as soon as the former; therefore, it becomes a four-day fever. 

In the three-day fevers, the struggle is most intense because the spirits of the heart combat the bilious humor, which is inherently hot in itself; for this reason, they are also called **hot fevers** <note>original: "hizige Fieber"</note>. That a weakness follows after the fever stems from the fact that the vital spirits, after the battle has been fought, leave the external limbs and retreat into rest.

That food is harmful during the <term>Paroxysm; the period of most intense symptoms or the "fit" of a fever</term> is because the **vital spirits** at that time do not "cook" <note>the process of digestion was historically viewed as a form of internal heat-based cooking or "concoction"</note> but must prepare themselves for battle. The spirit cannot do both things at once: either it only half-cooks the food and attacks the illness more weakly, or it leaves the food entirely uncooked; but if it tries to do both at once, it becomes far too weakened. The thick vapors arising from these produce flatulence; if these vapors are driven together into the head, they cause a catarrh <note>original: "Abtriefung"; a discharge or "dripping" of fluids from the head, often linked to colds or sinus issues</note>, and in other limbs a blockage; from this, rot or inflammation finally follows. Therefore, he who guards himself against <term>crudities</term> preserves his whole body from illness; this is achieved, however, through a moderate diet and daily physical exercise. Labor, therefore, possesses a singular power, in that we gain not only health but also our daily bread through it, and preserve what we have gained.

### §. 30.
## On the Diseases of the Mind.

1. These are nothing other than the vices which cause restlessness or pain to the mind <note>original: "Gemüthe"; refers to the emotional and spiritual inner-self, often translated as "mind," "soul," or "disposition"</note>; these vices are nothing other than the corrupt desires to live, to eat, to drink, to multiply, to know much, to possess much, and to have great honor. The actual names by which these vices are called and expressed are: self-love, intemperance, lust, meddlesome curiosity <note>original: "Fürwitz"; an over-eager or improper desire to know things that do not concern one</note>, greed, and ambition; for those who are devoted to such vices are always craving and restless.

### §. 32.
## On the Correspondence of All Things.

1. All that has been said in this entire work is nothing other than a correspondence <term>Uebereinstimmung: agreement, harmony, or the Hermetic principle that different levels of reality reflect one another</term> of the inner with the outer, the upper with the lower, and the spiritual with the bodily. Nor do I doubt, my brothers, that you will sufficiently understand this without further report. Nevertheless, in order to provide a guide therein, I will report only a little more regarding this in conclusion.

2. All creatures are entirely different according to their form, but according to their matter <note>original: "Materie"; refers to the underlying substance of which things are made</note> they are not much different from one another; therefore they have a great kinship among themselves.

3. The elements, because they all originate from one Chaos <term>Chaos: the primal, unformed state of matter from which God created the world</term>, have no other distinction among themselves than according to the manner of their disposition <term>Disponirung: the specific arrangement or ordering of the elements</term> and elevation <term>Erhöhung: a term often used in alchemy to describe the refinement or spiritualization of a substance</term>. All things are enclosed in Unity, and return there once again. This contemplation...

<summary>This page introduces the principle of universal correspondence, arguing that all things—spiritual and physical, high and low—are interconnected and share a common origin in a primal state of matter. It suggests that while forms vary, the underlying substance of all creation maintains a fundamental kinship and moves toward a final unity.</summary>
<keywords>correspondence, unity of nature, chaos, elements, spiritual vs bodily, macrocosm and microcosm, kinship of creatures</keywords>

If you further observe the birth, nourishment, and destruction or dissolution <term>Aufschließung: literally "opening up"; in alchemy, this refers to breaking down a substance into its components</term> of the three kingdoms <note>The "three kingdoms" of nature: animal, vegetable, and mineral</note>, you will see that in this matter they are all in agreement and coincide with one another. They take their growth from the three fundamental principles of nature, where the active part is the man, and the passive <note>original: "Leidende"; literally "the suffering" or "the enduring," referring to the receptive principle</note> is the woman. ### Of the first laboratory work and the better preparation of Mercury. <note>original symbol: ☿</note>

## Of the Third Work, and the Better Preparation 
## of the Little Birds.

The little birds <note>original Latin: *Avicularum*; in alchemy, "birds" often refer to volatile substances or precipitates that "fly" up during distillation</note> are indeed prepared rightly and well according to the instruction received in the Constitution, and the prepared mercury <note>symbol: ☿; also known as quicksilver</note> can be quite suitably animated with them; however, they presuppose a well-practiced fire-worker <note>an alchemist skilled in managing furnace temperatures</note>, because they are exceedingly prone to burning and hardening. One can, however, avoid this difficulty in the following way:

> " Melt 4 ounces <note>symbol: ℥ iiij</note> of our antimony <note>symbol: 🜗; specifically the Regulus of Antimony</note> with 
> " 2 ounces <note>symbol: ℥ ij</note> of fine cupelled silver <note>symbol: ☽ r</note>, and while in a 
> " good flow <note>in a molten state</note>, add a little of the aforementioned flux 
> " made of common fused salt <note>symbol: ⊝ com. fus</note> and refined water <note>symbol: 🜔 r.</note> 
> " upon it; let it [stand/react] for a quarter hour <note>symbol: 1/4 ⌛</note> 
> " according to our manner <note>abbreviation: m. n. a.; likely *more nostro alchemico*</note> in the crucible <note>symbol: ♁en</note>; 
> " then pour it out, wash the antimony-
> " silver <note>symbol: 🜗 ☽ nae</note> until it is most pure, 
> " dry it, and grind <note>symbol: 🜿 rten</note> it to the finest degree." 

By this means, one does not need to make any "little birds," but rather proceeds directly to the manipulation:

28. However, to understand the fundamental reason that moved our wise masters to <term>amalgamate: to mix or combine with mercury (originally 'ââiren')</term> our fiery mercury <note>symbol: ☿</note> with physical gold <note>symbol: ☉</note> in order to shorten the Work, one must only take into precise consideration both natural constituents, along with the immutable laws of the movement of nature. 

The gold <note>symbol: ☉</note> namely consisted initially of no other parts than those which constitute the essence of our fiery mercury <note>symbol: ☿</note>, and is distinguished from it in its solid, physical form only to this extent: that the mercury is still raw and immature, while the gold—by means of the length of time, the movement of nature, and its active principle—has ripened into a solid form and reached perfect <term>fixation: the chemical process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to heat</term>.

29. It is therefore, in respect to the <term>animated mercury: mercury that has been 'quickened' or prepared to be chemically active</term> <note>original Latin: *mercurii animati*</note>, precisely what a grain of wheat or any vegetable seed is in respect to the <term>Helmontian Leffas: a term used by the physician-chemist Jan Baptista van Helmont (1580–1644) to describe the fertile, life-giving soil or the vital matrix of the earth</term>—

30. “This *Leffas* <note>A term from the chemist Van Helmont referring to the vital, fertile essence of the earth or a watery matrix</note> is that water of the earth which contains the pure <term>salt of the earth: a fundamental mineral essence believed to be the basis of plant life</term> within itself. It provides nourishment and substance to the roots, branches, twigs, leaves, fruits, and seeds of all vegetation. Within these, it concentrates, brings to maturity, and encloses the <term>saltpeter: or niter, viewed by alchemists as a vehicle for the universal life-force</term> and the <term>sulfurous spirit: the active, combustible principle of nature</term> of nature with which it is impregnated—being the offspring of all four elements. It likewise encloses that very ray of light which, acting as the growth-promoting force, works back upon the same *Leffas* from which it originated. As soon as the shells, husks, and other hard parts of the seed grain (in which this force is bound) have been softened and dissolved by the *Leffas*, it forges a new body for itself according to the character of its natural imprint; finally, in a short time, it multiplies itself in power and number through the many new seed grains it produces. In a nearly identical manner, the spirit of light imprisoned within physical gold acts within our fiery <term>mercury: the chemical symbol ☿ represents Mercury</term> as its own *Leffas*, as soon as the mercury softens its [the gold's] hard shell and

For this very reason and with this very intention, we taught above to throw the precipitate mixed with <term>gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> onto 10 parts of <term>mercury</term> <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note>,

# Experimental Instructions

-> or <-

Necessary preparatory processes for the **Philosophical Work** <note>the "Great Work" (Magnum Opus), the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, regarding how the **Radical and Universal Solvents** <note>original: "Radical- auch Universalmenstrua," referring to powerful solvents capable of reducing substances to their "root" or essential state</note> and **Resolvents** <note>substances used to dissolve or separate materials</note> must be prepared from the mineral, vegetable, and animal kingdoms.

---

### First Process.
<note>original: "Processus Imus"</note>

### On the Preparation of the Mineral Radical Solvent.

**Recipe:** Take 1 pound of an **aqua** <note>original: "🜄", in this context referring to a distilled mineral acid like aqua fortis</note> made from equal parts **vitriol** <note>original: "🜖", likely iron or copper sulfate</note> and **nitre** <note>original: "🜔", saltpeter</note>; pour it over 1 pound of Hungarian **vitriol** <note>original: "🜖 hungarici," a high-quality green or blue vitriol</note> that has been **calcined to whiteness** <note>heated until it loses its water of crystallization and turns into a white powder</note>. Place it into a **retort** <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</note> and distill the **aqua** <note>original: "🜄"</note> from it by slow degrees, so that the **vitriol** is not [further] calcined, but only [heated] slowly up to the third degree in a **sand bath** <note>original: "🜀", a container of sand heated to provide even, gradual warmth to the vessel</note>. When the **aqua** has been distilled over, pour 1 pound of fresh **aqua** <note>original: "🜄"</note> into it, and pour it all back together onto the **vitriol** in the retort; let them **dissolve** <note>original: "solviren"</note> and **digest** <note>original: "digeriren," a process of gentle heating to encourage chemical reaction</note> together for 1 **day** <note>original: "🝽"</note>.
