# Process III.
## On the Preparation of the Animal Radical Solvent.

Recipe <note>original: R</note>: Take as much urine <note>original symbol: $\square$</note> from a healthy human as you wish, put it into a flask with a helm and receiver <note>the "helm" is the top of the still; the "receiver" or *Berlage* is the vessel that catches the distillate</note> well-sealed. Place it in the water bath <note>original: Balneum Mariae</note> at the first degree of heat; let it stand and <term>putrefy</term> <note>a stage of fermentation where the matter breaks down through gentle warmth</note> for 14 days <note>original symbol: ☾; while this symbol often represents a lunar month, in this context of putrefaction, it likely refers to a 14-day cycle</note>. Then, distill everything that will come over from the water bath <note>original: Balneo maris</note> by degrees; save that liquid. 

Now take the flask out, put the remaining matter into a retort in a <term>sand bath</term> <note>original symbol: $\therefore$ (Arena)</note> and once more drive the distillation by slow degrees <note>original: perlentos gradus</note>. First, a <term>phlegm</term> <note>the watery, tasteless part of a distillate</note> comes over. This is followed by a very sharp-tasting <term>spirit</term> <note>original symbol: $\sim$</note>, which is the animal spirit <note>original symbol: $\text{\textdagger}$</note>. After this follows a stinking, thick oil <note>original: $\circ \circ$ crassum</note>. Finally, there follows a matter burnt to coals at the bottom <note>original: in Fundo</note>, which is the alkaline part. 

Take out this matter burnt to coals, grind the thick oil <note>original: $\circ \circ$</note> into it, and let it <term>digest</term> <note>warm slowly to encourage chemical union</note> in the water bath for 1 month <note>original symbol: ☾</note>. Then pour the animal spirit <note>original symbol: $\text{\textdagger}$</note> together with the phlegm into it as well; let it digest again for 1 month, and distill it down to the third part. Pour that which has come over back in again,

# Instruction on the Great Mystery
## Description,

->That is: The Preparation of the Mineral Stone in the Dry Way. From Philosophical Discipline with the Harmony of the Brothers of the Golden and Rosy Cross <note>original Latin: *Roseae Aureae Crucis*; referring to the Rosicrucian Order</note>.<-

### First Labor.

### On the Preparation of the Living Mercury <note>original: *☿ vivi Mercurii*; "living" mercury refers to quicksilver in its active, metallic state before being "killed" or fixed in a chemical reaction</note>.

Take: 1 pound <note>℔</note> of Hungarian Antimony <note>original symbol: ♁; "Hungarian" antimony was highly prized for its purity in early modern metallurgy</note>, and also 1 pound of Living Mercury <note>☿ vivo</note>; grind them well together in a stone or glass mortar, and put the mixture into a coated vessel <note>the symbol ♁ is used here to represent the vessel, likely a retort or crucible made for antimony work</note>, which must be so large that only a fourth part is filled with the mass. Place this in a convenient distillation furnace that has no sand-bath <note>original: *Sandcapelle*; a container of sand used to distribute heat evenly. The absence of one implies a need for more direct or intense heat</note>, is nicely round, and into which the retort fits well; seal the joints with lute <note>original: *Laimen*; a paste made of clay, dung, or hair used to make alchemical apparatus airtight</note> so that no flames can lick around the glass, and also set a large receiver <note>a glass vessel used to collect the vapors that condense during distillation</note> in front, and

Repeat this process one more time, and your Regulus <term>🜫</term> will be well prepared.

# 5th Degree of the R. C. <note>original: "R. C."; refers to the Rosicrucians (Rose Cross), a secret society of mystics and alchemists</note>

These salts, of whatever species they may be, whether from the vegetable, mineral, or animal kingdom, differ among themselves only in their specific mixture; at their core, they all originate from the same base, which is the universal <term>Salt</term> <note>original symbol: ⊖</note> of Nature. This is what both the ancients and the moderns have recognized, and it is the reason why they have rejected none of these salts, working with each according to its own nature.

### III. Observation

9. # Corrections.

Page 8, Line 9: read **invisible powers**; instead of invisible humans.

Page 49, Line 6: read <term>Principle</term> <note>original Latin: *Principium*; refers to a fundamental source, beginning, or constituent element in alchemical philosophy</note>; instead of "Peinigium."

Page 68, Line 11: —— **Banquet**; instead of "Panquet."

Page 85, Line 10: —— <term>Soul</term> <note>original Hebrew: נֶפֶשׁ (*Nephesh*); a term often used in alchemical and mystical texts to describe the vital life force or the "animating spirit" of a substance</note>; instead of נֶבֶשׁ <note>a typographical error where the Hebrew letter 'Pe' was replaced with 'Bet'</note>.

Page 95, Line 3: —— <term>by rectifying</term> <note>original Latin: *rectificando*; the process of purifying a liquid, typically through repeated distillation, to reach its most potent state</note>; instead of "by ratifying" <note>original Latin: *ratificando*</note>.

Page 169, Line 17: —— <term>Matrix</term> <note>original Latin: *Matrice*; in a laboratory context, this refers to the "womb" or vessel where a substance is nurtured and transformed</note>; instead of "Matrise."

Page 180, Line 15: —— **Constellation**; instead of "Constilation."
